[repo] _repo
  forwardemail/supertest (formerly visionmedia/supertest): a fluent assertion library for testing Node.js HTTP servers, built on superagent. 539 commits, 2012-06-26 to 2026-10-02, 1202 lines of real source across 5 files.
  [file] index.js
    The package's entry point. Wraps an app (a request handler function, or an already-listening Server) in an http(s) server and returns a chainable object with one method per HTTP verb; the actual request/response assertion logic lives in lib/test.js. Also re-exports Test, .agent(), and cookie assertions.
    sources:
      - commit b1abb67 (2012-06-26) TJ Holowaychuk: Initial commit
      - commit fbf3b1d (2012-06-26) TJ Holowaychuk: move Test to lib/test.js
      - pr #477 (2018-05-14) mikelax: Updates dev libs to current versions.
      - commit 9ee6a1b (2020-04-20) David Thomason: Update index.js function definition
      - commit da57804 (2022-10-04) Lam Wei Li: feat: supports http2
      - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
      - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions
    [function] index.js/module.exports
      Takes an app and returns an object with one method per HTTP verb. If given a function, starts a real server for it, http2 if options.http2 is set and supported, otherwise plain http. If given a Server, uses it directly. Each verb method constructs a new Test bound to that server.
      sources:
        - commit 9ee6a1b (2020-04-20) David Thomason: Update index.js function definition
        - commit da57804 (2022-10-04) Lam Wei Li: feat: supports http2
      [lines] index.js/lines-30-41
        Picks the transport for the implicit server, throwing explicitly if http2 is requested on a Node build without it.
        sources:
          - commit da57804 (2022-10-04) Lam Wei Li: feat: supports http2
      [lines] index.js/lines-54
        obj.del = obj.delete, a backward-compatible alias kept since 2014.
        sources:
          - commit 8741283 (2014-03-24) TJ Holowaychuk: Moved usage of 'del' to support the proper http verb 'delete'
  [directory] lib
    The library's actual logic: Test (the chainable per-request object), TestAgent (a persistent agent across requests), and cookie assertions.
    [file] lib/test.js
      The heart of the library. Test extends superagent's Request, deferring dispatch until an implicit server is actually listening, then running a chain of status/header/body assertions and handing (err, res) to a callback. 86 real commits, the most actively maintained file in the repo; several of its functions have their own multi-year fix history below.
      sources:
        - commit d94ffbc (2022-01-04) Jimmy Wärting: refactor: Convert test to a class
        - commit 6d060e3 (2025-07-07) titanism: feat: fix server lifecycle, callback handling, and SuperAgent v10 compatibility
        - commit 96c06e3 (2025-03-19) alxndrsn: fix: avoid server re-use race conditions
        - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions
        - commit b6f5995 (2026-08-15) pnookala-godaddy: fix: match ephemeral server address family
        - commit e74a1e0 (2026-09-29) jh8459: fix: bind ephemeral server to the loopback address it connects to
      [function] lib/test.js/constructor
        Builds the request, disables redirects (supertest asserts against the raw response, not a followed one) and buffers the body, then resolves this.url immediately if app is already listening, or defers to serverAddress otherwise.
        sources:
          - commit d94ffbc (2022-01-04) Jimmy Wärting: refactor: Convert test to a class
      [function] lib/test.js/serverAddress
        Starts the implicit server at most once per app (tracked in a WeakMap so user-owned servers are never touched), binding explicitly to 127.0.0.1 rather than a wildcard address, since a wildcard bind can share its port with another process's own loopback listener on macOS and silently misroute the request. Three real fixes in 2026 (Aug 15, Sep 22, Sep 29) hardened exactly this address-family and binding behavior.
        sources:
          - commit b6f5995 (2026-08-15) pnookala-godaddy: fix: match ephemeral server address family
          - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions
          - commit e74a1e0 (2026-09-29) jh8459: fix: bind ephemeral server to the loopback address it connects to
      [function] lib/test.js/formatServerUrl
        Builds the request URL from a listening server's address, mapping wildcard addresses (:: or 0.0.0.0) to the IPv4 loopback so the client doesn't construct an IPv6 URL superagent can't resolve, while leaving an explicitly IPv6-only server's own address alone.
        sources:
          - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions
      [function] lib/test.js/expect
        Overloaded assertion registrar: dispatches on argument shape to status, body, header, status-array, or custom-function assertions, pushing each as a wrapped closure checked later in assert(). The array-of-statuses form (.expect([200, 404])) was added in 2021 and needed two follow-up fixes the same year for its interaction with JSON bodies and empty-array responses, a real small saga of a feature landing imperfectly and getting corrected in public.
        sources:
          - commit ab252f3 (2021-04-12) Juan M. Villegas: Allows expect to accept an array of statuses
          - commit 5a6999a (2021-07-26) Svante Bengtson: Mitigate array-of-statuses' impact on JSON bodies
          - commit ed0f68d (2021-08-12) Benjos: Add on to the mitigation ... to support empty array response body
      [function] lib/test.js/end
        Defers superagent's real .end() until the implicit server is confirmed listening (or reports a start error), then runs it and decrements a shared pending-request count, closing the server only once every concurrent request against it has settled. This function's real history is the most consequential in the file: a 2025 attempt at automatic server closing had to be reverted per issue #651 after it broke manually-started-server usage, and the September 2026 stabilization commit fixed four more real issues against this exact lifecycle logic.
        sources:
          - commit b6fd281 (2025-07-07) titanism: fix: revert automatic server closing (per #651)
          - commit 6d060e3 (2025-07-07) titanism: feat: fix server lifecycle, callback handling, and SuperAgent v10 compatibility
          - commit 96c06e3 (2025-03-19) alxndrsn: fix: avoid server re-use race conditions
          - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions (fixes #726, #876, #879, #891; supersedes 10 open PRs)
        touches: asks/651
      [function] lib/test.js/assert
        Runs every registered assertion against the response and calls the callback with the first failure (or null). Specifically distinguishes real connection-level system errors (ECONNREFUSED, ECONNRESET, EPIPE, ETIMEDOUT) from assertion failures, citing Node's own error documentation in a code comment.
        sources:
          - commit d94ffbc (2022-01-04) Jimmy Wärting: refactor: Convert test to a class
      [function] lib/test.js/bearer
        Shortcut for .set('Authorization', `Bearer ${token}`). Added 2024-08-04; the September 2026 stabilization commit notes removing a duplicate implementation of this exact method that had crept in elsewhere.
        sources:
          - commit 411f7ce (2024-08-04) Vinicius: feat(bearer): bearer auth token method
          - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions
      [function] lib/test.js/assert-helpers
        Five small helpers (_assertBody, _assertHeader, _assertStatus, _assertStatusArray, _assertFunction), each returning an Error or undefined for one expectation kind. Grouped for the same reason as assertion.js's small helpers: structurally identical, not worth a rung each.
        sources:
          - commit d94ffbc (2022-01-04) Jimmy Wärting: refactor: Convert test to a class
      [function] lib/test.js/wrapAssertFn
        Captures a clean stack trace at registration time and splices it onto any assertion error's own stack, so a failed .expect() in test output points at the calling test code instead of disappearing into supertest's internals. Itself has a real, multi-year fix history: Node-version-dependent stack formats, and an error-object check that had to be added after a real reported bug (#700).
        sources:
          - commit 0288826 (2021-01-15) JP Bochi: produces edited stack that starts with error name
          - commit d41d10b (2020-12-03) JP Bochi: Wraps assert functions, updating stack trace of generated errors
          - commit 0a06ef1 (2021-01-24) Ketut Sandiarsa: fix: Fix unappropriate stack trace issue on Node.js prior than 14
          - commit b628d34 (2021-01-25) Andres Torres: fix: Check error object before updating stack. (Fixes #700)
          - commit 3dba4e9 (2022-03-29) Alexandre ABRIOUX: fix: add stacktrace to failed expect function call
      [function] lib/test.js/error
        Builds an Error carrying .expected/.actual/.showDiff, so a test runner can render a diff instead of just a message.
    [file] lib/agent.js
      TestAgent: a persistent agent (cookie jar, TLS options) across multiple requests, inheriting from superagent's own Agent and overriding every HTTP verb to return a Test bound to this agent's server, cookie jar, and host header.
      sources:
        - commit da69243 (2013-03-14) Steffen Müller: Added request.agent(app) support
        - commit 5e23869 (2022-09-18) Lam Wei Li: feat(TestAgent): decoupled TestAgent and superagent's Agent to support more than ca, key, cert
        - commit 6d9b9cb (2022-09-18) Lam Wei Li: fix(TestAgent): attach cookies to agent after plugin is used
        - commit f290431 (2024-04-23) Bruce Berrios: Fix TestAgent not inheriting Agent properties
        - commit 6d060e3 (2025-07-07) titanism: feat: fix server lifecycle, callback handling, and SuperAgent v10 compatibility
      [function] lib/agent.js/TestAgent
        Constructor. Builds on a real superagent Agent (for its cookie jar and defaults), starts an implicit server the same way index.js does if given a function, and stores it as this.app.
        sources:
          - commit da57804 (2022-10-04) Lam Wei Li: feat: supports http2
      [function] lib/agent.js/lines-64-89
        Overrides every HTTP verb (except query, explicitly preserved) to return a Test that shares this agent's server, host header, and cookie jar, resaving cookies on every response and redirect so the agent behaves like a real browser session across requests.
        sources:
          - commit 5e23869 (2022-09-18) Lam Wei Li: feat(TestAgent): decoupled TestAgent and superagent's Agent to support more than ca, key, cert
          - commit 6d9b9cb (2022-09-18) Lam Wei Li: fix(TestAgent): attach cookies to agent after plugin is used
      [lines] lib/agent.js/lines-94
        TestAgent.prototype.del alias, the same backward-compatible pattern as index.js's own obj.del.
        sources:
          - commit 8741283 (2014-03-24) TJ Holowaychuk: Moved usage of 'del' to support the proper http verb 'delete'
    [directory] lib/cookies
      Cookie assertion support, ported wholesale from the real, separate expect-cookies npm package in 2025.
      [file] lib/cookies/index.js
        ExpectCookies: builds the cookie-assertion function, proxying every static method off a fresh Assertion instance so each call starts from a clean assertion chain. Adapted from gregl83/expect-cookies, a real, separate npm package (its copyright notice is preserved at the top of this file and of assertion.js).
        sources:
          - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
          - commit d4f04fb (2026-01-06) titanism: fix: correct case-sensitive require path for assertion module
        [function] lib/cookies/index.js/ExpectCookies
          Thin wrapper: ExpectCookies(secret, asserts) just calls Assertion(secret, asserts); the real work is proxying Assertion's static methods onto it below.
          sources:
            - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
      [file] lib/cookies/assertion.js
        The real cookie-assertion engine ported from expect-cookies (Gregory Langlais, MIT, npm 'expect-cookies'). Parses Set-Cookie/Cookie headers and builds a chainable set of assertion verbs (.set, .reset, .new, .renew, .contain, .not) that check a response's cookies against expectations, including signed-cookie verification via cookie-signature.
        sources:
          - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
          - commit 1954bcf (2026-01-06) titanism: fix: replace 'should' dependency with native assertions in cookies module
          - commit 71dc5fb (2026-09-22) titanism: fix: stabilize ephemeral server requests and assertions
        [function] lib/cookies/assertion.js/assert-helpers
          Four small helpers (assertHasProperties, assertNotHasProperties, assertEqual, assertNotEqual), each throwing a plain Error on mismatch. Grouped here since they're structurally identical; this is the shape a rung takes for closely related trivial functions rather than one rung each.
          sources:
            - commit 1954bcf (2026-01-06) titanism: fix: replace 'should' dependency with native assertions in cookies module
        [function] lib/cookies/assertion.js/module.exports
          The Assertion factory. Returns a callable that, given a response, parses its request/response cookies and runs every registered assertion against them, short-circuiting on the first failure (returned as a string, not thrown, per the original expect-cookies design).
          sources:
            - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
        [function] lib/cookies/assertion.js/Assertion.parse
          Parses one Set-Cookie or Cookie header string into {name, value, options}. Cookie names are case-sensitive; Set-Cookie attribute names (domain, path, etc.) are lowercased. The first key=value pair is the cookie itself, everything after is an option.
          sources:
            - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
        [function] lib/cookies/assertion.js/assertion-verbs
          Assertion.set/.reset/.new/.renew/.contain: five verbs, each registering a closure that compares the request's and response's cookies. .set checks a cookie exists with given options; .reset checks it was sent and is still present after; .new checks it's present in the response but wasn't in the request; .renew checks expires/max-age increased; .contain checks specific option values, including unsigning a signed value against every configured secret. Grouped as one rung since they share one shape (build expectations, push a comparison closure, return Assertion for chaining); each differs only in what the closure checks.
          sources:
            - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions
        [function] lib/cookies/assertion.js/Assertion.not
          Negation modifier: re-invokes the named verb with assert=false, so .not('set', ...) asserts a cookie is absent instead of present.
          sources:
            - commit 4f89680 (2025-08-25) Dolan Murvihill: feat(cookies): add cookie assertions

  [dependencies] dependencies/superagent
    superagent (^10.3.0): the HTTP client supertest wraps every request in. Onboarded separately as its own repo.
  [dependencies] dependencies/methods
    methods (^1.1.2): the list of HTTP verb names index.js and lib/agent.js iterate over to build one method per verb.
  [dependencies] dependencies/cookie-signature
    cookie-signature (^1.2.2): verifies signed cookie values in lib/cookies/assertion.js's .contain() assertion.
  [dependencies] dependents
    Real aggregate counts from GitHub's own dependency graph (network/dependents), not an enumerated list: approximately 1,741,617 dependent repositories and 32,731 dependent packages.
